F.3.3
Deriving MSSP in Regime II

This subsection presents the MSSP-Regime-II derivation per Definition
2
, with the user-specified modification
W_{0}^{4}=0
. MSSP-Regime-II differs from
\mu
P-Regime-II (Definition
1
) in exactly two places: the variance of
W_{0}^{3,i}
is
M/N_{e}
(entries
\Theta(\sqrt{N})
) rather than
1/N_{e}
(entries
\Theta(1)
); and
W_{0}^{4}=0
rather than variance
1/N^{2}
. All other init variances, learning rates, gating, and standing assumptions are identical to
\mu
P-Regime-II.

Two structural consequences propagate through the derivation:

1.

The boosted
W_{0}^{3,i}
variance gives
h_{0}^{3,i}
entries of scale
\Theta(\sqrt{N})
(vs
\Theta(1)
in
\mu
P), which after
1/M
-aggregation with the cross-
i
CLT gives
h_{0}^{3}
entries
\Theta(1)
. So the aggregated activation is at the leading scale at init, even though the prediction
f_{0}=0
vanishes.

2.

Since
W_{0}^{4}=0
, every gradient at
t=0
that carries a
W_{0}^{4}
factor is zero, so only
W^{4}
updates at
t=1
. Hidden weights first move at
t=2
. Three forward + three backward passes are needed to capture all post-update correlations.

Compared to
\mu
P-Regime-II: MSSP-Regime-II’s larger
W^{3,i}
init means the bottleneck-layer expert weights start big and the per-expert hidden state
h^{3,i}
inherits that scale; updates to
W^{3,i}
are then sub-leading relative to its init (vs comparable in
\mu
P-Regime-II). The aggregated
h^{3}
still feature-learns at
\Theta(1)
, just through a different mechanism: the prediction reaches
f_{1}\in\Theta(1)
at
t=1
purely from the
W^{4}\cdot h_{0}^{3}
contraction (since
h_{0}^{3}\in\Theta(1)
entries,
\|h_{0}^{3}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N)
), with no hidden updates required.

Setup and standing assumptions

Architecture.

Same as in §
F.3.1
.

Initialization.

Per Definition
2
with
W_{0}^{4}=0
:

\displaystyle(W_{0}^{1})_{ab}

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1/D),

\displaystyle(Q_{0})_{ia}

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1/N),

\displaystyle(W_{0}^{2,i})_{ab}

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1/N),

\displaystyle(W_{0}^{3,i})_{ab}

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,M/N_{e}),

\displaystyle W_{0}^{4}

\displaystyle=0.

All parameters drawn independently across
i
. Resulting entry scales:
W_{0}^{4}=0
,
W_{0}^{2,i}\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N})
,
W_{0}^{3,i}\in\Theta(\sqrt{M})=\Theta(\sqrt{N})
,
W_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1)
,
Q_{0}\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N})
.

Learning rates (SGD).

Same as
\mu
P-Regime-II:
\eta_{1}=\eta N
,
\eta_{Q}=\eta M/N
,
\eta_{2}=\eta M/N
,
\eta_{3}=\eta MN
,
\eta_{4}=\eta/N
.

Gating.

Sigmoid +
1/M
aggregation:
\phi_{i,t},\dot{\phi}_{i,t}\in\Theta(1)
.

Auxiliary scaling lemmas

We reuse CLT, LLN, and Lemma
7
.

Specialization of Lemma
7
to MSSP-Regime-II.
For
W=W_{0}^{3,i}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N_{e}}
with
\sigma_{W}^{2}=M/N_{e}
and
N_{e}=\Theta(1)
:

•

W_{0}^{3,i}(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
has rank
\leq N_{e}=\Theta(1)
. Diagonal mean
n\sigma^{2}=M=\Theta(N)
, off-diagonal entries variance
n\sigma^{4}=M^{2}/N_{e}=\Theta(N^{2})
, coordinate scale
\Theta(N)
.
Every entry is
\Theta(N)
— much larger than
\mu
P-Regime-II’s
\Theta(1)
entries.

•

(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}W_{0}^{3,i}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{e}\times N_{e}}
, rank-
N_{e}
full. Diagonal mean
m\sigma^{2}=NM/N_{e}=\Theta(N^{2})
, off-diagonal coord scale
\Theta(\sigma^{2}\sqrt{m})=\Theta(N^{3/2})
. So acting on a
\Theta(1)
-coordinate
N_{e}
-vector
h_{0}^{2,i}
gives entries of coordinate scale
\Theta(N)\cdot\Theta(1)+\Theta(N^{3/2})\cdot\Theta(1)=\Theta(N^{2})
at leading.

The other Gram products (
W_{0}^{2,i}
versions) are unchanged from
\mu
P-Regime-II.

First forward pass

h_{0}^{1}=W_{0}^{1}\,x\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{1}^{2}=1/D,\ \|x\|^{2}\in\Theta(D))

\psi_{0}=Q_{0}\,h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{Q}^{2}=1/N,\ \|h_{0}^{1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N))

\phi_{i,0}=\sigma(\psi_{i,0})\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{sigmoid bounded; gating assumption})

\displaystyle h_{0}^{2,i}

\displaystyle=W_{0}^{2,i}\,h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1),\quad\text{independent across }i

\displaystyle\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{2}^{2}=1/N,\ \|h_{0}^{1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N);

\displaystyle\qquad\|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}=N_{e}\cdot\Theta(1)=\Theta(1)\text{ since }N_{e}=\Theta(1)).

(R2
s
-F1.4)

\displaystyle h_{0}^{3,i}

\displaystyle=W_{0}^{3,i}\,h_{0}^{2,i}\in\Theta(\sqrt{N}),\quad\|h_{0}^{3,i}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N^{2})\quad(\textbf{boosted by MSSP variance})

(R2
s
-F1.5)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{3}^{2}=M/N_{e}=\Theta(N);\ \|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.4)};

\displaystyle\qquad\text{variance per entry }\Theta(N)\cdot\Theta(1)=\Theta(N))

\displaystyle h_{0}^{3}

\displaystyle=(1/M)\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,0}\,h_{0}^{3,i}\in\Theta(1)

(R2
s
-F1.6)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{cross-$i$ CLT on }h_{0}^{3,i}\text{ entries }\Theta(\sqrt{N})\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.5)};

\displaystyle\qquad\{h_{0}^{3,i}\}\text{ independent across }i;

\displaystyle\qquad\text{variance per entry }(1/M^{2})\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,0}^{2}\,\Theta(N)=\Theta(N/M)=\Theta(1);

\displaystyle\qquad\sqrt{M}\text{ cross-}i\text{ CLT cancellation balances the }\sqrt{M}\text{ amplification of }h_{0}^{3,i})

f_{0}=W_{0}^{4}\,h_{0}^{3}=0.\quad(W_{0}^{4}=0\text{ by MSSP-Regime-II standing assumption})

First backward pass and step-1 updates

By the chain rule, every hidden gradient at
t=0
(
\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{3}
,
\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{3,i}
,
\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{2,i}
,
\partial f_{0}/\partial\phi_{i,0}
, and both expert and router contributions to
\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{1}
) carries a
W_{0}^{4}=0
factor and is therefore zero. Hence
W_{1}^{\ell}=W_{0}^{\ell}
for
\ell\in\{1,Q,(2,i),(3,i)\}
; only
W^{4}
is updated at
t=1
.

Step-1 parameter update.

\Delta_{1}W^{4}=-(\eta\chi_{0}/N)\,(h_{0}^{3})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).\quad(h_{0}^{3}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.6)})

Second forward pass

Since the non-readout weights are unchanged at
t=1
, every hidden activation is unchanged:
h_{1}^{\ell}=h_{0}^{\ell}
for
\ell\in\{1,(2,i),(3,i),3\}
,
\psi_{1}=\psi_{0}
,
\phi_{1}=\phi_{0}
.

f_{1}=W_{1}^{4}\,h_{0}^{3}=-\tfrac{\eta\chi_{0}}{N}\,\|h_{0}^{3}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1).\quad(\|h_{0}^{3}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N)\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.6)})

Second backward pass and step-2 updates

New intermediate at
t=1
.

\displaystyle(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}h_{0}^{3}

\displaystyle\in\Theta(N)\text{ entry-wise}

\displaystyle\quad(\text{using (R2$s$-F1.6)}\text{ to expand }h_{0}^{3}=(1/M)\sum_{j}\phi_{j,0}h_{0}^{3,j};

\displaystyle\qquad j=i\text{ piece }(\phi_{i,0}/M)(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}W_{0}^{3,i}h_{0}^{2,i}\text{ with}

\displaystyle\qquad(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}W_{0}^{3,i}h_{0}^{2,i}\in\Theta(N^{2})\text{ from Lemma~\ref{lem:ssp_gram} specialization},

\displaystyle\qquad\text{giving }(\phi_{i,0}/M)\cdot\Theta(N^{2})=\Theta(N);

\displaystyle\qquad j\neq i\text{ pieces give cross-$j$ noise of comparable }\Theta(N)\text{ scale})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\ (W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}

\displaystyle=-(\eta\chi_{0}/N)(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}h_{0}^{3}\in\Theta(1)\text{ entry-wise}

\displaystyle\quad(W_{1}^{4}\text{ via (R2$s$-U1.4)})

Per-layer gradients.

\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{3}=(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).\quad(\text{via (R2$s$-U1.4)})

\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{3,i}=(\phi_{i,0}/M)(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N^{2}).\quad(\phi_{i,0}\in\Theta(1);\ M=\Theta(N))

\displaystyle\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{2,i}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{i,0}/M)(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N)

(R2
s
-B2.3)

\displaystyle\quad((W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via the new intermediate above})

\displaystyle\partial f_{1}/\partial\phi_{i,0}

\displaystyle=(1/M)\langle h_{0}^{3,i},W_{1}^{4}\rangle=-(\eta\chi_{0}/(MN))\langle h_{0}^{3,i},h_{0}^{3}\rangle\in\Theta(1/N)

(R2
s
-B2.4)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{Four-piece grid: only (init}\cdot\text{update) is non-zero at }t=1,

\displaystyle\qquad\text{since }W_{0}^{4}=0\text{ kills the *}\cdot\text{init column}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{and }\Delta_{1}W^{3,i}=0\text{ (hidden weights frozen) kills the update}\cdot\text{* row;}

\displaystyle\qquad(\text{init}\cdot\text{update})\ \langle(W_{0}^{3,1})\,h_{0}^{2,i},\Delta_{1}W^{4}\rangle\in\Theta(1):

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\text{expanding }h_{0}^{3}=(1/M)\sum_{j}\phi_{j,0}h_{0}^{3,j}\text{ gives}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\langle h_{0}^{3,i},h_{0}^{3}\rangle=(\phi_{i,0}/M)\|h_{0}^{3,i}\|^{2}+(1/M)\textstyle\sum_{j\neq i}\phi_{j,0}\langle h_{0}^{3,i},h_{0}^{3,j}\rangle;

\displaystyle\qquad\quad j=i\text{ diagonal term: }(\phi_{i,0}/M)\|h_{0}^{3,i}\|^{2}=(1/M)\Theta(N^{2})=\Theta(N)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\text{using }\|h_{0}^{3,i}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N^{2})\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.5)};

\displaystyle\qquad\quad j\neq i\text{ off-diagonal terms give comparable }\Theta(N)\text{ via cross-}j\text{ CLT};

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\text{so }\langle h_{0}^{3,i},h_{0}^{3}\rangle\in\Theta(N);

\displaystyle\qquad\text{substituting: }\partial f_{1}/\partial\phi_{i,0}=-(\eta\chi_{0}/(MN))\cdot\Theta(N)=\Theta(1/M)=\Theta(1/N);

\displaystyle\qquad\text{lifted from }\mu\text{P-R2's (init}\cdot\text{update)}=\Theta(1/N^{2})\text{ entry}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{by the boost }\sigma_{3}^{2}=M/N_{e})

\displaystyle(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}

\displaystyle=(1/M)\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,0}(W_{0}^{2,i})^{\top}(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N)

(R2
s
-B2.5)

\displaystyle\quad((W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via the new intermediate above};

\displaystyle\qquad(W_{0}^{2,i})^{\top}\text{ acts on a }\Theta(1)\text{ vector with }\|v\|^{2}=N_{e}\Theta(1)=\Theta(1);

\displaystyle\qquad\text{variance per entry }(1/N)\Theta(1)=\Theta(1/N),\text{ coord }\Theta(1/\sqrt{N})\text{ random per }i;

\displaystyle\qquad\text{cross-$i$ CLT: }\Theta(1/N))

\displaystyle(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{router}}

\displaystyle=(1/M)Q_{0}^{\top}v,\quad v_{i}:=\dot{\phi}_{i,0}\langle h_{0}^{3,i},W_{1}^{4}\rangle\in\Theta(1/N)

(R2
s
-B2.6)

\displaystyle\quad(v_{i}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-B2.4)};\ \|v\|^{2}=M\Theta(1)=\Theta(M);

\displaystyle\qquad Q_{0}^{\top}v\text{ entries variance }(1/N)\Theta(M)=\Theta(1)\text{ via cross-layer CLT};

\displaystyle\qquad(1/M)\Theta(1)=\Theta(1/N);

\displaystyle\qquad\text{W}^{3,i}\text{-split }v_{i}=v^{I}_{i}+v^{U}_{i}\text{ where}

\displaystyle\qquad v^{I}_{i}:=\dot{\phi}_{i,0}\langle(W_{0}^{3,1})h_{0}^{2,i},W_{1}^{4}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad v^{U}_{i}:=\dot{\phi}_{i,0}\langle(\Delta_{1}W^{3,i})h_{0}^{2,i},W_{1}^{4}\rangle=0\text{ since }\Delta_{1}W^{3,i}=0;

\displaystyle\qquad v^{I}_{i}\in\Theta(1)\text{ coherent via boost (cf.\ R2$s$-B2.4)};

\displaystyle\qquad(1/M)Q_{0}^{\top}v^{I}=\Theta(1/N)\text{ and }(1/M)Q_{0}^{\top}v^{U}=0)

\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1}=(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}+(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{router}}\in\Theta(1/N).

Step-2 parameter updates.

\Delta_{2}W^{4}=-(\eta\chi_{1}/N)(h_{0}^{3})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).\quad(h_{0}^{3}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.6)})

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}W^{3,i}

\displaystyle=-\eta N\chi_{1}\phi_{i,0}\,(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}(h_{0}^{2,i})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)\text{ rank-1}

(R2
s
-U2.3)

\displaystyle\quad((W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N)\text{ via (R2$s$-U1.4)};\ h_{0}^{2,i}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.4)};

\displaystyle\qquad\text{sub-leading vs }W_{0}^{3,i}\in\Theta(\sqrt{N})\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.5)})

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}W^{2,i}

\displaystyle=-(\eta\chi_{1}\phi_{i,0}/N)(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}(h_{0}^{1})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N)\text{ rank-1}

(R2
s
-U2.2)

\displaystyle\quad((W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via the new intermediate};

\displaystyle\qquad\text{sub-leading vs }W_{0}^{2,i}\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N}))

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}W^{1}

\displaystyle=-\eta_{1}\chi_{1}(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})\,x^{\top}\in\Theta(1)

(R2
s
-U2.1a)

\displaystyle\quad(\eta_{1}=\eta N;\ \partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1}\in\Theta(1/N)\text{ via (R2$s$-B2.7)})

\Delta_{2}h^{1}=\Delta_{2}W^{1}\,x\in\Theta(1),\text{ aligned along }h_{0}^{1}.

\Delta_{2}Q\in\Theta(1/N).\quad(\partial f_{1}/\partial\phi\in\Theta(1/N)\text{ via (R2$s$-B2.4)})

Third forward pass

h_{2}^{1}=h_{0}^{1}+\Delta_{2}h^{1}\in\Theta(1).\quad(\Delta_{2}h^{1}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-U2.1b)};\ h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1))

(R2
s
-F3.2)
h_{2}^{2,i}=W_{2}^{2,i}h_{2}^{1}
, four-piece decomposition:

h_{2}^{2,i}=h_{0}^{2,i}+W_{0}^{2,i}\Delta_{2}h^{1}+\Delta_{2}W^{2,i}h_{0}^{1}+\Delta_{2}W^{2,i}\Delta_{2}h^{1}.

\text{init: }h_{0}^{2,i}\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{R2$s$-F1.4})

\displaystyle\text{prop: }W_{0}^{2,i}\Delta_{2}h^{1}

\displaystyle\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\sigma_{2}^{2}\|\Delta_{2}h^{1}\|^{2}=(1/N)\Theta(N)=\Theta(1);

\displaystyle\qquad\|\Delta_{2}h^{1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N)\text{ via (R2$s$-U2.1b)}).

(R2
s
-F3.2b)

\displaystyle\text{eff: }\Delta_{2}W^{2,i}h_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle=-(\eta\chi_{1}\phi_{i,1}/N)\|h_{0}^{1}\|^{2}(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{(R2$s$-U2.2)substitution};

\displaystyle\qquad(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via the new intermediate at }t=1;

\displaystyle\qquad\|h_{0}^{1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N)).

(R2
s
-F3.2c)

\displaystyle\text{cross: }\Delta_{2}W^{2,i}\Delta_{2}h^{1}

\displaystyle=-(\eta\chi_{1}\phi_{i,1}/N)\langle h_{0}^{1},\Delta_{2}h^{1}\rangle(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad((W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via the new intermediate};

\displaystyle\qquad\langle h_{0}^{1},\Delta_{2}h^{1}\rangle\in\Theta(N)\text{ coherent via (R2$s$-U2.1b)}).

(R2
s
-F3.2d)

\Delta_{2}h^{2,i}\in\Theta(1)
entry-wise.

(R2
s
-F3.3)
h_{2}^{3,i}=W_{2}^{3,i}h_{2}^{2,i}
, four-piece decomposition:

h_{2}^{3,i}=h_{0}^{3,i}+W_{0}^{3,i}\Delta_{2}h^{2,i}+\Delta_{2}W^{3,i}h_{0}^{2,i}+\Delta_{2}W^{3,i}\Delta_{2}h^{2,i}.

\text{init: }h_{0}^{3,i}\in\Theta(\sqrt{N}).\quad(\text{R2$s$-F1.5})

\displaystyle\text{prop: }W_{0}^{3,i}\Delta_{2}h^{2,i}

\displaystyle\in\Theta(\sqrt{N})

\displaystyle\quad(\sigma_{3}^{2}\|\Delta_{2}h^{2,i}\|^{2}=\Theta(N)\cdot\Theta(1)=\Theta(N);

\displaystyle\qquad\|\Delta_{2}h^{2,i}\|^{2}=N_{e}\Theta(1)=\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-F3.2)};\ N_{e}=\Theta(1)).

(R2
s
-F3.3b)

\displaystyle\text{eff: }\Delta_{2}W^{3,i}h_{0}^{2,i}

\displaystyle=-\eta N\chi_{1}\phi_{i,1}\|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{(R2$s$-U2.3)substitution};

\displaystyle\qquad\|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.4)};

\displaystyle\qquad(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N)\text{ via (R2$s$-U1.4)}).

(R2
s
-F3.3c)

\text{cross: }\Delta_{2}W^{3,i}\Delta_{2}h^{2,i}\text{ tracked as part of }D\text{ in (R2$s$-F3.4)}.

Propagating dominates
:
\Delta_{2}h^{3,i}\in\Theta(\sqrt{N})
— same scale as
h_{0}^{3,i}
at init. The effective piece
\Theta(1)
is sub-leading per-expert but contributes coherently to the aggregation in (R2
s
-F3.4d).

(R2
s
-F3.4)
h_{2}^{3}=A_{1}+A_{2,1}+A_{2,2}+A_{3}+D
, where

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle:=\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,2}h_{0}^{3,i},

\displaystyle A_{2,1}

\displaystyle:=\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,2}W_{0}^{3,i}W_{0}^{2,i}\Delta_{2}h^{1},

\displaystyle A_{2,2}

\displaystyle:=\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,2}W_{0}^{3,i}\Delta_{2}W^{2,i}h_{0}^{1},

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle:=\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,2}\Delta_{2}W^{3,i}h_{0}^{2,i},

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle:=\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,2}\Delta_{2}W^{3,i}\Delta_{2}h^{2,i}.

A_{1}\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{cross-$i$ CLT, same calculation as (R2$s$-F1.6)})

\displaystyle A_{2,1}

\displaystyle\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{per summand }W_{0}^{3,i}W_{0}^{2,i}\Delta_{2}h^{1}\in\Theta(\sqrt{N})\text{ random};

\displaystyle\qquad\|\Delta_{2}h^{1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N)\text{ via (R2$s$-U2.1b)};

\displaystyle\qquad\text{cross-$i$ CLT: }\Theta(\sqrt{N}/\sqrt{M})=\Theta(1)).

(R2
s
-F3.4b)

\displaystyle A_{2,2}

\displaystyle\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{per summand }W_{0}^{3,i}[\Delta_{2}W^{2,i}h_{0}^{1}]\in\Theta(\sqrt{N})

\displaystyle\qquad\text{since }\Delta_{2}W^{2,i}h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-F3.2c)};

\displaystyle\qquad\text{cross-$i$ CLT: }\Theta(\sqrt{N}/\sqrt{M})=\Theta(1)).

(R2
s
-F3.4c)

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle=-\eta N\chi_{1}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,2}\phi_{i,1}\|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}\Bigr)\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{(R2$s$-U2.3)substitution};

\displaystyle\qquad\|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.4)};\ \text{LLN};

\displaystyle\qquad(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N)).

(R2
s
-F3.4d)

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=-\eta N\chi_{1}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\,\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,2}\phi_{i,1}\langle h_{0}^{2,i},\Delta_{2}h^{2,i}\rangle\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{(R2$s$-U2.3)substitution};

\displaystyle\qquad\langle h_{0}^{2,i},\Delta_{2}h^{2,i}\rangle\in\Theta(1)\text{ coherent across }i,

\displaystyle\qquad\text{from two pieces: }\Delta_{2}h^{1}\parallel h_{0}^{1}\text{ via (R2$s$-U2.1b)},

\displaystyle\qquad\text{and the }j=i\text{ Gram self-overlap in the effective piece (R2$s$-F3.2c)};

\displaystyle\qquad\text{LLN gives empirical average }\Theta(1)).

(R2
s
-F3.4e)

h_{2}^{3}\in\Theta(1)\text{ entry-wise}.

\displaystyle f_{2}

\displaystyle=W_{2}^{4}\,h_{2}^{3}=-(\eta(\chi_{0}+\chi_{1})/N)\langle h_{0}^{3},h_{2}^{3}\rangle\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(W_{2}^{4}=W_{1}^{4}+\Delta_{2}W^{4}\text{ via (R2$s$-U2.4)};

\displaystyle\qquad\langle h_{0}^{3},h_{2}^{3}\rangle\in\Theta(N)\text{ coherent, dominated by }A_{3}+D\text{ via (R2$s$-F3.4)};

\displaystyle\qquad\text{net }\eta\cdot(1/N)\cdot\Theta(N)=\Theta(1)).

(R2

The structure parallels (R2

-B2.1)–(R2

W_{0}^{4}=0
in MSSP, the four “
.1
” pieces

— using
W_{0}^{4}
rather than
\Delta W^{4}
— are identically zero at every step; only the four “
.2

W_{2}^{4}=\Delta W^{4}
now non-zero (via (R2
s
-U2.4)), all gradients are non-zero.

Per-layer gradients.

\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{3}=(W_{2}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).\quad(\text{(R2$s$-U2.4)})

\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{3,i}=(\phi_{i,2}/M)(W_{2}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N^{2}).\quad(\phi_{i,2}\in\Theta(1);\ M=\Theta(N))

\displaystyle\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{2,i}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{i,2}/M)(W_{2}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{2}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N)

\displaystyle\quad((W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{2}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via the cumulative analog of the new intermediate at }t=1;

\displaystyle\qquad(\Delta_{2}W^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{2}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-U2.3)}).

Router pathway
\partial f_{2}/\partial\phi_{i,2}
.

\displaystyle\partial f_{2}/\partial\phi_{i,2}

\displaystyle=(1/M)\langle h_{2}^{3,i},W_{2}^{4}\rangle\in\Theta(1/N),

(R2
s
-B3.4)

\displaystyle\quad\text{Four-piece grid: }*\cdot\text{init column is identically zero }(W_{0}^{4}=0);

\displaystyle\quad(\text{init}\cdot\text{update})\ \langle(W_{0}^{3,1})\,h_{2}^{2,i},\Delta_{2}W^{4}\rangle\in\Theta(1)\text{: }

\displaystyle\qquad\langle h_{0}^{3,i},W_{2}^{4}\rangle=-\tfrac{\chi_{0}+\chi_{1}}{N}\langle h_{0}^{3,i},h_{0}^{3}\rangle,

\displaystyle\qquad\text{expanding }h_{0}^{3}=(1/M)\textstyle\sum_{j}\phi_{j,0}h_{0}^{3,j}\text{ gives the diagonal }j=i\text{ term}

\displaystyle\qquad(\phi_{i,0}/M)\|h_{0}^{3,i}\|^{2}=(1/M)\Theta(N^{2})=\Theta(N)\text{ via (R2$s$-F1.5)};

\displaystyle\qquad j\neq i\text{ off-diagonal terms give comparable }\Theta(N);

\displaystyle\qquad\text{so }\langle h_{0}^{3,i},W_{2}^{4}\rangle\in\Theta(1);

\displaystyle\quad(\text{update}\cdot\text{update})\ \langle(\Delta_{2}W^{3,i})\,h_{2}^{2,i},\Delta_{2}W^{4}\rangle\in\Theta(1)\text{: }

\displaystyle\qquad\langle(\Delta_{2}W^{3,i})\,h_{2}^{2,i},W_{2}^{4}\rangle=\kappa\langle(W_{1}^{4})^{\top},W_{2}^{4}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\text{with }\kappa=-\eta N\chi_{1}\phi_{i,1}\|h^{2,i}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N)

\displaystyle\qquad\text{and }\langle W_{1}^{4},W_{2}^{4}\rangle=(\chi_{0}(\chi_{0}+\chi_{1})/N^{2})\|h_{0}^{3}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1/N);

\displaystyle\qquad\text{net }\Theta(N)\cdot\Theta(1/N)=\Theta(1);

\displaystyle\quad\text{substituting: }\partial f_{2}/\partial\phi_{i,2}=(1/M)\bigl(\Theta(1)+\Theta(1)\bigr)=\Theta(1/M)=\Theta(1/N);

\displaystyle\quad\text{lifted from }\mu\text{P-R2's grid }(\Theta(1/N^{3/2}),\Theta(1/N^{3/2}),\Theta(1/N),\Theta(1/N))

\displaystyle\quad\text{to MSSP-R2's }(0,\Theta(1/N),0,\Theta(1/N))\text{ via }W_{0}^{4}=0\text{ and }\sigma_{3}^{2}=M/N_{e}.

.

\displaystyle\qquad\langle W_{1}^{4},h_{0}^{3,i}\rangle=-(\eta\chi_{0}/N)\langle h_{0}^{3},h_{0}^{3,i}\rangle\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\qquad\text{(coherent }j=i\text{ piece via }\|h_{0}^{3,i}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N^{2})\text{ from (R2$s$-F1.5)};

\displaystyle\qquad\text{plus }j\neq i\text{ random of comparable scale)};

\displaystyle\qquad\text{per summand entries }\Theta(1/N);

\displaystyle\qquad\text{cross-$i$ LLN on coherent piece: }\Theta(1/N)).

(R2

(\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}\in\Theta(1/N),\text{ all four non-zero pieces at leading scale}.

Router pathway.

\displaystyle(\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{1})_{\mathrm{router}}

\displaystyle=(1/M)Q_{2}^{\top}v,\quad v_{i}:=\dot{\phi}_{i,2}\langle h_{2}^{3,i},W_{2}^{4}\rangle\in\Theta(1/N)

\displaystyle\quad(v_{i}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R2$s$-B3.4)};

\displaystyle\qquad\text{by the analysis of (R2$s$-B2.6) extended to cumulative }Q_{2}=Q_{0}+\Delta_{2}Q;

\displaystyle\qquad\Delta_{2}Q\text{ piece coherent via (R2$s$-U2.Q)};

(R2

-B3.6)

W

-balance transmits through the router pathway.

-split of (R2

\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{v}^{I}+\mathbf{v}^{U}

\Theta(1)

and

\mu

\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{1}=(\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}+(\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{1})_{\mathrm{router}}\in\Theta(1/N).

F.4
Scaling derivation for Regime III

In this section, we provide the heuristic scaling derivation for Regime III. Throughout we work under the proportional limit
N\asymp N_{e}\asymp M\asymp K\to\infty
.

Definition 0

(
\mu
P baseline, Regime III)
.

•

Initialization.
All parameters are drawn
independently
according to:

W_{0}^{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,D^{-1}),\;Q_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,N^{-1}),\;W_{0}^{2,i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,N^{-1}),\;

W_{0}^{3,i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,N_{e}^{-1}),\;W_{0}^{4}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,N^{-2}),\;\text{for }i\in[M].

•

SGD learning rates.

\eta_{1}=\eta\,N
,
\;\eta_{Q}=\eta
,
\;\eta_{2}=\eta_{3}=\eta\,M
,
\;\eta_{4}=\eta\,N^{-1}
.

•

Adam learning rates.

\eta_{1}=\eta
,
\;\eta_{Q}=\eta_{2}=\eta_{3}=\eta_{4}=\eta\,N^{-1}
.

•

Adam epsilon.

\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon\,N^{-1}
,
\;\epsilon_{Q}=\epsilon\,M^{-1}
,
\;\epsilon_{2}=\epsilon_{3}=\epsilon\,N^{-1}M^{-1}
,
\;\epsilon_{4}=\epsilon
.

Definition 0

(Maximally Scale-Stable Parameterization (MSSP), Regime III)
.

MSSP adopts the same per-layer initialization variances and learning rate scalings as the
\mu
P baseline (Definition
3
), with the sole exception that expert weights are
shared across experts at initialization
: a single pair
(W_{0}^{2},W_{0}^{3})
is sampled and assigned to every expert, such that

W_{0}^{2,i}=W_{0}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad W_{0}^{3,i}=W_{0}^{3}\quad\text{for all }i=1,\dots,M.

F.4.1
Compound aggregate lemmas in Regime III

The analyses in this section invoke three compound aggregate-level results stating how sums across
M
independent experts of products and Gram products of the per-expert weight matrices behave under proportional scaling. All three are exact moment computations under Gaussian initialization, with independence across experts and from the input vector
v
as the only inputs.

Lemma 0

(Cross-layer product sum across experts)
.

Let
\{W^{2,i}\}_{i=1}^{M}
have i.i.d. centered Gaussian entries of variance
\sigma_{2}^{2}=1/N
in
\mathbb{R}^{N_{e}\times N}
, and let
\{W^{3,i}\}_{i=1}^{M}
have i.i.d. centered Gaussian entries of variance
\sigma_{3}^{2}=1/N_{e}
in
\mathbb{R}^{N\times N_{e}}
, with all matrices mutually independent across
i
and across the two layers. Let
c_{i}\in\Theta(1)
be approximately independent of these weights, and let
v\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
have entries of coordinate scale
\Theta(\alpha)
, independent of all
W^{2,i},W^{3,i}
. Then

G\;:=\;\frac{1}{M}\sum_{i=1}^{M}c_{i}\,(W^{2,i})^{\top}(W^{3,i})^{\top}\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}

has zero mean, entries of coordinate scale
\Theta(1/\sqrt{NM})
, and
Gv
has entries of coordinate scale
\Theta(\alpha/\sqrt{M})=\Theta(\alpha/\sqrt{N})
.

Proof.

\mathbb{E}[G_{a,b}]=(1/M)\sum_{i}c_{i}\,\mathbb{E}[\sum_{k}W^{2,i}_{k,a}W^{3,i}_{b,k}]=0
by independence across the two layers. Variance:

\mathrm{Var}(G_{a,b})=\frac{1}{M^{2}}\sum_{i}c_{i}^{2}\,\mathrm{Var}\!\Bigl(\sum_{k}W^{2,i}_{k,a}W^{3,i}_{b,k}\Bigr)=\frac{1}{M^{2}}\sum_{i}c_{i}^{2}\cdot N_{e}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}=\Theta\!\Bigl(\tfrac{N_{e}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}}{M}\Bigr)=\Theta\!\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{MN}\Bigr).

Cross-entry covariances
\mathbb{E}[G_{a,b_{1}}G_{a,b_{2}}]
for
b_{1}\neq b_{2}
vanish by independence, so

\mathrm{Var}((Gv)_{a})=\sum_{b}\mathrm{Var}(G_{a,b})\,v_{b}^{2}=N\cdot\Theta(1/(MN))\cdot\alpha^{2}=\Theta(\alpha^{2}/M)
.
∎

Lemma 0

(Gram concentration across experts)
.

Let
\{W^{a,i}\}_{i=1}^{M}
have i.i.d. centered Gaussian entries of variance
\sigma^{2}
in
\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
, independent across
i
. Let
c_{i}\in\Theta(1)
be approximately independent of the weights, with empirical mean
\bar{c}
. Let
G^{a,i}
denote either Gram
(W^{a,i})^{\top}W^{a,i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
or
W^{a,i}(W^{a,i})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
, and let
v
be a vector of compatible dimension at coordinate scale
\Theta(\alpha)
, independent of all
W^{a,i}
. Then

\frac{1}{M}\sum_{i=1}^{M}c_{i}\,G^{a,i}\,v=\bar{c}\,(\mathrm{tr\text{-}dim}\cdot\sigma^{2})\,v+r,\qquad r\text{ at coordinate scale }\Theta(\sigma^{2}\sqrt{mn/M}\,\alpha),

where
\mathrm{tr\text{-}dim}
is
m
for the inner Gram
((W^{a,i})^{\top}W^{a,i})
and
n
for the outer Gram
(W^{a,i}(W^{a,i})^{\top})
.

For the specific instances used in this section:

•

Inner Gram:
W^{2,i}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{e}\times N}
with
\sigma_{2}^{2}=1/N
gives leading constant
m\sigma^{2}=N_{e}/N
;
W^{3,i}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N_{e}}
with
\sigma_{3}^{2}=1/N_{e}
gives leading constant
m\sigma^{2}=N/N_{e}
.

•

Outer Gram: both layers give
n\sigma^{2}=1
.

All leading constants are
\Theta(1)
under proportional scaling, with random correction
\Theta(\alpha/\sqrt{N})
.

Proof.

Inner Gram case:
\mathbb{E}[G_{j,k}]=(1/M)\sum_{i}c_{i}\,\mathbb{E}[\sum_{a}W^{a,i}_{a,j}W^{a,i}_{a,k}]=\bar{c}\,m\sigma^{2}\,\delta_{j,k}
, with off-diagonal variance
\Theta(m\sigma^{4}/M)
giving coordinate scale
\Theta(\sigma^{2}\sqrt{m/M})
and off-diagonal contribution to
(Gv)_{j}-\bar{c}m\sigma^{2}v_{j}
at coordinate scale
\Theta(\sigma^{2}\sqrt{mn/M}\,\alpha)
. Outer Gram case is the same statement with the roles of
m
and
n
swapped, equivalently obtained by applying the inner-Gram result to
W^{\top}
(whose entries are i.i.d. Gaussian of the same variance with the dimension pair swapped).
∎

F.4.2
Deriving
\mu
P in Regime III

This subsection provides a self-contained derivation of the heuristic width scaling for the
\mu
P baseline (Definition
3
) in Regime III. We invoke the compound aggregate lemmas of §
F.4.1
for sums across independent experts of weight-product structures, and use CLT/LLN inline for single matrix-vector products and gating averages.

The principal structural consequence is the following. Although hidden weights move at
t=1
(since
W_{0}^{4}\neq 0
in
\mu
P), the embedding update
\Delta_{1}h^{1}
comes out at
\Theta(1/\sqrt{N})
, not
\Theta(1)
, because both the expert and router contributions to
\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{1}
cancel across independent experts via CLT. Full
\Theta(1)
feature learning at the embedding emerges at
t=2
, when the rank-one updates
\Delta_{1}W^{2,i},\Delta_{1}W^{3,i}
inject coherent contributions (
A_{5},A_{6}
) into the expert pathway gradient that align across experts along
(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}
and so escape the cross-
i
cancellation. We therefore analyse three forward and three backward passes.

First forward pass

h_{0}^{1}=W_{0}^{1}\,x\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{1}^{2}=1/D,\ \|x\|^{2}\in\Theta(D))

\psi_{0}=Q_{0}\,h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{Q}^{2}=1/N,\ \|h_{0}^{1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N))

\phi_{0}=\sigma(\psi_{0})\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{sigmoid bounded; gating assumption})

h_{0}^{2,i}=W_{0}^{2,i}\,h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1),\text{ indep.\ across }i.\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{2}^{2}=1/N,\ \|h_{0}^{1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N))

h_{0}^{3,i}=W_{0}^{3,i}\,h_{0}^{2,i}\in\Theta(1),\text{ indep.\ across }i.\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{3}^{2}=1/N_{e},\ \|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N_{e}))

h_{0}^{3}=(1/M)\sum_{i}\phi_{i,0}\,h_{0}^{3,i}\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{M})=\Theta(1/\sqrt{N}).\quad(\text{cross-}i\text{ CLT})

f_{0}=W_{0}^{4}\,h_{0}^{3}=\langle W_{0}^{4},h_{0}^{3}\rangle\in\Theta(1/N).\quad(\text{CLT};\ W_{0}^{4}\text{ entries }\Theta(1/N)\text{ indep.\ of }h_{0}^{3})

First backward pass and step-1 updates

\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{3}=(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).

\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{3,i}=(\phi_{i,0}/M)(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/(MN)).

\displaystyle\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{2,i}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{i,0}/M)(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/(MN)).

(R3
\mu
-B1.3)

\displaystyle\quad((W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N)\text{ by CLT};\ \sigma_{3}^{2}=1/N_{e})

\displaystyle\partial f_{0}/\partial\phi_{i,0}

\displaystyle=(1/M)\langle h_{0}^{3,i},W_{0}^{4}\rangle\in\Theta(1/(M\sqrt{N}))=\Theta(1/N^{3/2}).

(R3
\mu
-B1.4)

\displaystyle\quad(\langle h_{0}^{3,i},W_{0}^{4}\rangle\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N})\text{ by CLT})

\displaystyle\Bigl(\tfrac{\partial f_{0}}{\partial h_{0}^{1}}\Bigr)_{\!\!\mathrm{exp}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,0}\,(W_{0}^{2,i})^{\top}(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}

\displaystyle\in\Theta\bigl(\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}\sqrt{N_{e}}\,\|(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}\|\,/\sqrt{M}\bigr)\text{ random direction}\quad(\text{Lem.~\ref{lem:r3_cross_layer}; cross-$i$ CLT})

\displaystyle=\Theta(1/\sqrt{N}\cdot 1/\sqrt{N_{e}}\cdot\sqrt{N_{e}}\cdot 1/\sqrt{N}\cdot 1/\sqrt{M})=\Theta(1/N^{3/2}).

(R3
\mu
-B1.5)

\displaystyle\Bigl(\tfrac{\partial f_{0}}{\partial h_{0}^{1}}\Bigr)_{\!\!\mathrm{router}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{M}Q_{0}^{\top}v,\qquad v_{i}:=\dot{\phi}_{i,0}\langle W_{0}^{4},h_{0}^{3,i}\rangle

\displaystyle\in\Theta\bigl(\sigma_{Q}\|v\|/M\bigr)\text{ random direction}\quad(Q_{0}\text{ indep.\ of }v;\ \text{cross-layer CLT})

\displaystyle=\Theta(1/\sqrt{N}\cdot 1\cdot 1/N)=\Theta(1/N^{3/2})

(R3
\mu
-B1.6)

\displaystyle\quad(\sigma_{Q}^{2}=1/N;\ v_{i}\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N})\text{ random across }i\Rightarrow\|v\|^{2}\in\Theta(1)).

\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{1}=(\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}+(\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{1})_{\mathrm{router}}\in\Theta(1/N^{3/2}).

Step-1 parameter updates.

\Delta_{1}W^{4}=-\tfrac{\chi_{0}}{N}(h_{0}^{3})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N^{3/2}).\quad(h_{0}^{3}\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N})\text{ by (R3$\mu$-F1.6)})

\Delta_{1}W^{3,i}=-\eta\chi_{0}\phi_{i,0}\,(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}(h_{0}^{2,i})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).\quad(\eta_{3}=\eta M)

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}W^{2,i}

\displaystyle=-\eta\chi_{0}\phi_{i,0}\,(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}(h_{0}^{1})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).

(R3
\mu
-U1.2)

\displaystyle\quad((W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N)\text{ by (R3$\mu$-B1.3)})

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}W^{1}

\displaystyle=-\eta N\chi_{0}\,(\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{1})\,x^{\top}\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N}).

(R3
\mu
-U1.1a)

\displaystyle\quad(\eta_{1}=\eta N,\ \partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1/N^{3/2})\text{ by (R3$\mu$-B1.7)})

\Delta_{1}h^{1}=\Delta_{1}W^{1}\,x\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N}),\text{ sub-leading vs }h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1).

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}Q

\displaystyle=-\eta_{Q}\chi_{0}\,\mathrm{diag}(\dot{\phi}_{0})\,(\partial f_{0}/\partial\phi)\,(h_{0}^{1})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N^{3/2}).

(R3
\mu
-U1.Q)

\displaystyle\quad(\partial f_{0}/\partial\phi\in\Theta(1/N^{3/2})\text{ by (R3$\mu$-B1.4)};\ \eta_{Q}=\eta)

Second forward pass

h_{1}^{1}=h_{0}^{1}+\Delta_{1}h^{1}\in\Theta(1).\quad(h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1)\text{ dominates }\Delta_{1}h^{1}\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N})\text{ by (R3$\mu$-U1.1b)})

\displaystyle\psi_{1}

\displaystyle=\psi_{0}+\Delta_{1}\psi\in\Theta(1),\quad\phi_{1}\in\Theta(1).

(R3
\mu
-F2.2–R3
\mu
-F2.3)

\displaystyle\quad(\Delta_{1}\psi\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N})\text{ from }Q_{0}\Delta_{1}h^{1}\text{ and }\Delta_{1}Q\,h_{0}^{1}\text{; scales by (R3$\mu$-U1.1b), (R3$\mu$-U1.Q)})

(R3
\mu
-F2.4)
h_{1}^{2,i}=W_{1}^{2,i}\,h_{1}^{1}

\text{init: }W_{0}^{2,i}h_{0}^{1}=h_{0}^{2,i}\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{R3$\mu$-F1.4})

Op-norm of iid Gaussian

(R3

(R3
\mu
-F2.5)
h_{1}^{3,i}=W_{1}^{3,i}\,h_{1}^{2,i}
, four-piece decomposition:

-F2.6)
h_{1}^{3}=A_{1}+A_{2,1}+A_{2,2}+A_{3}+D
, where

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle=-\eta\chi_{0}\,\|h_{0}^{1}\|^{2}\,\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}\phi_{i,0}\,W_{0}^{3,i}(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle=-\eta\chi_{0}\,(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}\,\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}\phi_{i,0}\,\|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}\Bigr)\in\Theta(1)\text{ along }(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}.

(R3
\mu
-F2.6d)

\displaystyle\quad(\|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N_{e});\ \text{LLN})

\displaystyle\in\Theta\bigl(\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}\phi_{i,0}\,\langle h_{0}^{2,i},\Delta_{1}h^{2,i}\rangle\bigr)\cdot(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}\text{ along }(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad(\text{cross-$i$ CLT: }\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\text{ random across }i,\text{ mean }0)

\displaystyle=\Theta(\sqrt{N}/\sqrt{M}\cdot 1/N)=\Theta(1/N)\text{ along }(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}

(R3
\mu
-F2.6e)

\displaystyle\quad(\langle h_{0}^{2,i},\Delta_{1}h^{2,i}\rangle\in\Theta(\sqrt{N});\ (W_{0}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N);\ M\in\Theta(N))

h_{1}^{3}\in\Theta(1)\text{ along }(W_{0}^{4})^{\top},\text{ from }A_{2,2}+A_{3}.

\displaystyle W_{0}^{4}\,h_{1}^{3}

\displaystyle=\langle W_{0}^{4},A_{2,2}+A_{3}\rangle+\cdots\in\Theta(1).

(R3
\mu
-F2.7a)

\displaystyle\quad(A_{2,2},A_{3}\text{ aligned along }(W_{0}^{4})^{\top};\ \langle W_{0}^{4},A_{3}\rangle=-\eta\chi_{0}\|W_{0}^{4}\|^{2}\bar{c}\,N_{e}\in\Theta(1))

\Delta_{1}W^{4}\,h_{1}^{3}=-(\chi_{0}/N)\langle h_{0}^{3},h_{1}^{3}\rangle\in\Theta(1/N).

f_{1}=W_{1}^{4}\,h_{1}^{3}\in\Theta(1).

Second backward pass and step-2 updates

\displaystyle\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{3}

\displaystyle=(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}=(W_{0}^{4})^{\top}+(\Delta_{1}W^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).

(R3

-B2.1)

\displaystyle\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{2,i}

(R3

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\phi_{i,1}\in\Theta(1);\ M\in\Theta(N))

\displaystyle\partial f_{1}/\partial\phi_{i,1}

\displaystyle=(1/M)\langle h_{1}^{3,i},W_{1}^{4}\rangle\in\Theta(1/M).

(R3
\mu
-B2.4)

\displaystyle\quad(\langle h_{1}^{3,i},W_{0}^{4}\rangle=-\eta\chi_{0}\phi_{i,0}\|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}\|W_{0}^{4}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via eff.\ term of (R3$\mu$-F2.5)};

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\|h_{0}^{2,i}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N_{e});\ \|W_{0}^{4}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1/N))

(R3
\mu
-B2.5)
(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}=A_{4}+A_{5}+A_{6}+E
, where

\displaystyle A_{4}

\displaystyle:=\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}(W_{0}^{2,i})^{\top}(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top},

\displaystyle:=\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}(W_{0}^{2,i})^{\top}(\Delta_{1}W^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top},

\displaystyle:=\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}(\Delta_{1}W^{2,i})^{\top}(\Delta_{1}W^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}.

\displaystyle A_{5}

\displaystyle=-\eta\chi_{0}\,\|W_{0}^{4}\|^{2}\,\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}\phi_{i,0}\,(W_{0}^{2,i})^{\top}W_{0}^{2,i}\,h_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle\in\Theta\bigl(\|W_{0}^{4}\|^{2}\,\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}\phi_{i,0}\bigr)\cdot h_{0}^{1}\text{ along }h_{0}^{1}\quad(\text{Lem.~\ref{lem:r3_gram}})

\displaystyle=\Theta(1/N\cdot 1\cdot 1)=\Theta(1/N)\text{ along }h_{0}^{1}

(R3

-B2.5b)

\displaystyle A_{6}

\displaystyle=-\eta\chi_{0}\,h_{0}^{1}\,\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}\phi_{i,0}\,W_{0}^{4}W_{0}^{3,i}(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}

\displaystyle\in\Theta\bigl(\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}\phi_{i,0}\,\|W_{0}^{4}\|^{2}\bigr)\cdot h_{0}^{1}\text{ along }h_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle\quad(\text{Lem.~\ref{lem:r3_gram}: }W_{0}^{3,i}(W_{0}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\to(W_{1}^{4})^{\top};\ \langle W_{0}^{4},(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\rangle\to\|W_{0}^{4}\|^{2})

\displaystyle=\Theta(1\cdot 1/N)=\Theta(1/N)\text{ along }h_{0}^{1}

(R3
\mu
-B2.5c)

\displaystyle\quad(\|W_{0}^{4}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1/N);\ \text{LLN};\ h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1))

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}(\Delta_{1}W^{2,i})^{\top}(\Delta_{1}W^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}\chi_{0}^{2}\,h_{0}^{1}\,\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,1}\phi_{i,0}^{2}\,\langle W_{0}^{4},h_{0}^{3,i}\rangle\,(h_{0}^{3})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\quad(\text{substituting (R3$\mu$-U1.2),(R3$\mu$-U1.3)})

\displaystyle\in\Theta\bigl(\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum_{i}\langle W_{0}^{4},h_{0}^{3,i}\rangle\bigr)\cdot h_{0}^{1}\text{ along }h_{0}^{1}\quad(\text{cross-$i$ CLT: }\langle W_{0}^{4},h_{0}^{3,i}\rangle\text{ random across }i,\text{ mean }0)

\displaystyle=\Theta(1/\sqrt{N}\cdot 1/\sqrt{M}\cdot\|h_{0}^{3}\|^{2}/N)=\Theta(1/N^{2})\text{ along }h_{0}^{1}

(R3
\mu
-B2.5d)

\displaystyle\quad(\langle W_{0}^{4},h_{0}^{3,i}\rangle\in\Theta(1/\sqrt{N})\text{ by (R3$\mu$-B1.4)};\ \|h_{0}^{3}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1);\ M\in\Theta(N))

(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}\in\Theta(1/N)\text{ along }h_{0}^{1}.

\displaystyle(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{router}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{M}Q_{0}^{\top}v,\qquad v_{i}:=\dot{\phi}_{i,1}\langle h_{1}^{3,i},W_{1}^{4}\rangle

\displaystyle\in\Theta\bigl(\sigma_{Q}\|v\|/M\bigr)\text{ random direction}

\displaystyle\quad(Q_{0}\text{ indep.\ of }v;\ \text{cross-layer CLT};\ \Delta_{1}Q\text{ sub-leading by (R3$\mu$-U1.Q)})

\displaystyle=\Theta(1/\sqrt{N}\cdot\sqrt{M}\cdot 1/M)=\Theta(1/\sqrt{MN})=\Theta(1/N)

(R3
\mu
-B2.6)

\displaystyle\quad(\sigma_{Q}^{2}=1/N;\ v_{i}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R3$\mu$-B2.4)}\Rightarrow\|v\|^{2}\in\Theta(M);\ M\in\Theta(N))

\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1}=(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}+(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{router}}\in\Theta(1/N),

the proper feature-learning rate, an order of magnitude larger than
\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{1}
.

. The cumulative embedding change

\quad E_{2}=(1/M)\sum_{i}\phi_{i,t}\,(\Delta_{t}W^{2,i})^{\top}(\Delta_{t}W^{3,i})^{\top}(\Delta_{t}W^{4})^{\top}

0

\Theta(1/N^{2})

\Theta(1/\sqrt{MN})

\Theta(1/(MN))

F.4.4
Deriving MSSP in Regime III

This subsection provides a self-contained, heuristic scaling analysis for the Maximally Scale-Stable Parameterization (MSSP, Definition
4
) in Regime III.

Auxiliary scaling lemma

Lemma 0

(Gram concentration)
.

Let
W\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
have i.i.d. centered entries of variance
\sigma_{W}^{2}
. Then

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(W^{\top}W)_{jk}\bigr]=m\sigma_{W}^{2}\,\delta_{jk},\qquad\mathbb{E}\bigl[(WW^{\top})_{ij}\bigr]=n\sigma_{W}^{2}\,\delta_{ij},

with off-diagonal fluctuations of order
\sigma_{W}^{2}\sqrt{m}
(resp.
\sigma_{W}^{2}\sqrt{n}
). For any fixed
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
\|v\|^{2}\in\Theta(n)
that is independent of
W
,
W^{\top}W\,v=m\sigma_{W}^{2}\,v+r
with
r
of coordinate scale
\Theta(\sigma_{W}^{2}\sqrt{mn})
. The analogous statement holds for
WW^{\top}
acting on
v^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
with
\|v^{\prime}\|^{2}\in\Theta(m)
.

Sketch.

(W^{\top}W)_{jk}=\sum_{a}W_{aj}W_{ak}
is a sum of
m
centered i.i.d. products with mean
\sigma_{W}^{2}\delta_{jk}
and variance
\sigma_{W}^{4}
off-diagonal. The residual
r=(W^{\top}W-m\sigma_{W}^{2}I)v
has entry-wise variance
\sum_{k}m\sigma_{W}^{4}\,v_{k}^{2}=m\sigma_{W}^{4}\,\|v\|^{2}
, hence coordinate scale
\sigma_{W}^{2}\sqrt{mn}
.
∎

Specializations of Lemma
7
under proportional scaling.
Both Gram matrices of
W_{0}^{2,1}
and of
W_{0}^{3,1}
produce
\Theta(1)
-coordinate output when applied to
\Theta(1)
-coordinate input vectors (independent of or weakly correlated with the corresponding
W
):

\displaystyle(W_{0}^{2,1})^{\top}W_{0}^{2,1}\,v

\displaystyle=(N_{e}/N)\,v+r,

\displaystyle W_{0}^{2,1}(W_{0}^{2,1})^{\top}\,v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=v^{\prime}+r^{\prime},

\displaystyle(W_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}W_{0}^{3,1}\,u

\displaystyle=(N/N_{e})\,u+r^{\prime\prime},

\displaystyle W_{0}^{3,1}(W_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}\,u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=u^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime\prime},

where each residual is of the same
\Theta(1)
entry-wise order as the leading deterministic part. The Gram approximation is order-of-magnitude correct, with comparable random fluctuations.

First forward pass

h_{0}^{1}=W_{0}^{1}\,x\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{1}^{2}=1/D,\ \|x\|^{2}\in\Theta(D))

\psi_{0}=Q_{0}\,h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{Q}^{2}=1/N,\ \|h_{0}^{1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N))

\phi_{0}=\sigma(\psi_{0})\in\Theta(1).\quad(\text{sigmoid bounded; gating assumption})

\displaystyle h_{0}^{2,i}

\displaystyle=h_{0}^{2,1}=W_{0}^{2,1}\,h_{0}^{1}\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{2}^{2}=1/N,\ \|h_{0}^{1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N);\ h_{0}^{2,i}=h_{0}^{2,1}\text{ by MSSP}).

(R3
s
-F1.4)

\displaystyle h_{0}^{3,i}

\displaystyle=h_{0}^{3,1}=W_{0}^{3,1}\,h_{0}^{2,1}\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{CLT};\ \sigma_{3}^{2}=1/N_{e},\ \|h_{0}^{2,1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N_{e});\ h_{0}^{3,i}=h_{0}^{3,1}\text{ by MSSP}).

(R3
s
-F1.5)

\displaystyle h_{0}^{3}

\displaystyle=(1/M)\textstyle\sum_{i}\phi_{i,0}\,h_{0}^{3,1}=(\textstyle\sum_{k}\phi_{k,0}/M)\,h_{0}^{3,1}\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad((\textstyle\sum_{k}\phi_{k,0}/M)\in\Theta(1)\text{ by LLN}).

(R3
s
-F1.6)

f_{0}=W_{0}^{4}\,h_{0}^{3}=0.\quad(\text{standing assumption }W_{0}^{4}=0)

First backward pass and step-1 updates

By the chain rule, every hidden gradient (
\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{3}
,
\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{3,i}
,
\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{2,i}
,
\partial f_{0}/\partial\phi_{i,0}
,
\partial f_{0}/\partial h_{0}^{1}
via either the expert or router path) carries a
W_{0}^{4}=0
factor and is therefore zero. Hence every non-readout weight is unchanged at
t=1
:
W_{1}^{\ell}=W_{0}^{\ell}
for
\ell\in\{1,Q,2,3\}
.

Step-1 parameter updates.

\Delta_{1}W^{4}=-\tfrac{\chi_{0}}{N}\,(h_{0}^{3})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).\quad(\text{aligned along }(h_{0}^{3})^{\top})

Second forward pass

Since the non-readout weights are unchanged at
t=1
, every hidden activation is unchanged:
h_{1}^{\ell}=h_{0}^{\ell}
for
\ell\in\{1,(2,i),(3,i),3\}
,
\psi_{1}=\psi_{0}
,
\phi_{1}=\phi_{0}
.

f_{1}=W_{1}^{4}\,h_{0}^{3}=-\tfrac{\chi_{0}}{N}\|h_{0}^{3}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1).\quad(\|h_{0}^{3}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N)\text{ via (R3$s$-F1.6)})

Second backward pass and step-2 updates

\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{3}=(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}=(\Delta_{1}W^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).\quad(\text{(R3$s$-U1.4)};\ W_{0}^{4}=0)

\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{3,i}=(\phi_{i,0}/M)(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/(MN)).\quad(\phi_{i,0}\in\Theta(1))

\displaystyle\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{2,i}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{i,0}/M)(W_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/(MN))

\displaystyle\quad((W_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}=-\tfrac{\chi_{0}}{MN}(\textstyle\sum\phi)(W_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}W_{0}^{3,1}h_{0}^{2,1}\in\Theta(1/N)\text{ by Lem.~\ref{lem:ssp_gram}};

\displaystyle\qquad\phi_{i,0}\in\Theta(1)).

(R3
s
-B2.3)

\displaystyle\partial f_{1}/\partial\phi_{i,0}

\displaystyle=(1/M)(h_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/M)

\displaystyle\quad((h_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}=-\tfrac{\chi_{0}}{MN}(\textstyle\sum\phi)\|h_{0}^{3,1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(1)\text{ by LLN};

\displaystyle\qquad\|h_{0}^{3,1}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N_{e})\text{ via (R3$s$-F1.5)};\ N_{e}/N\in\Theta(1)\text{ in Regime III}).

(R3
s
-B2.4)

\displaystyle\Bigl(\tfrac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial h_{1}^{1}}\Bigr)_{\!\!\mathrm{exp}}

\displaystyle=\bigl(\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum\phi\bigr)(W_{0}^{2,1})^{\top}(W_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}

\displaystyle\in\Theta\bigl(\tfrac{1}{M}\textstyle\sum\phi\bigr)\cdot(W_{0}^{2,1})^{\top}h_{0}^{2,1}\,/N\text{ along }(W_{0}^{2,1})^{\top}h_{0}^{2,1}

\displaystyle\quad(\text{Lem.~\ref{lem:ssp_gram} on }(W_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}h_{0}^{3})

\displaystyle=\Theta(1\cdot 1/N)=\Theta(1/N)

\displaystyle\quad(\text{LLN};\ (W_{0}^{2,1})^{\top}h_{0}^{2,1}\in\Theta(1)\text{ by Lem.~\ref{lem:ssp_gram}}).

(R3
s
-B2.5)

\displaystyle\Bigl(\tfrac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial h_{1}^{1}}\Bigr)_{\!\!\mathrm{router}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{M}Q_{0}^{\top}v,\qquad v_{i}:=\dot{\phi}_{i,0}(h_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}(W_{1}^{4})^{\top}

\displaystyle\in\Theta\bigl(\sigma_{Q}\|v\|/M\bigr)\text{ random direction}\quad(Q_{0}\text{ indep.\ of }v;\ \text{cross-layer CLT})

\displaystyle=\Theta(1/\sqrt{N}\cdot\sqrt{M}\cdot 1/M)=\Theta(1/\sqrt{MN})

\displaystyle\quad(\sigma_{Q}^{2}=1/N;\ v_{i}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R3$s$-B2.4)}\Rightarrow\|v\|^{2}\in\Theta(M)).

(R3
s
-B2.6)

\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1}=(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}+(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})_{\mathrm{router}}\in\Theta(1/N).

Step-2 parameter updates.

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}W^{4}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{\chi_{1}}{N}(h_{1}^{3})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N),\quad W_{2}^{4}=-\tfrac{\chi_{0}+\chi_{1}}{N}(h_{0}^{3})^{\top}

(R3

-U2.4)

\Delta_{2}W^{2,i}=-\tfrac{\chi_{1}\phi_{i,0}}{N}\,(W_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}h_{0}^{3}\,(h_{0}^{1})^{\top}\in\Theta(1/N).\quad((W_{0}^{3,1})^{\top}h_{0}^{3}\in\Theta(1)\text{ via (R3$s$-B2.3)})

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}W^{1}

\displaystyle=-\eta_{1}\chi_{1}(\partial f_{1}/\partial h_{1}^{1})\,x^{\top}\in\Theta(1)

\displaystyle\quad(\eta_{Q}=1;\ \partial f_{1}/\partial\phi\in\Theta(1/M)\text{ via (R3$s$-B2.4)}).

(R3

-U2.Q)

We compute activations at

W_{2}^{\ell}=W_{0}^{\ell}+\Delta_{2}W^{\ell}

(R3

-F3.2)

\displaystyle\quad(\langle h_{0}^{3},h_{0}^{3,1}\rangle\in\Theta(N)\text{ coherent via (R3$s$-F1.6)};\ \|h_{0}^{3}\|^{2}\in\Theta(N)\text{ via (R3$s$-F1.6)}).

(R3

-B3.5d)

(\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}\in\Theta(1/N)\text{ along }h_{0}^{1}.

\displaystyle(\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{1})_{\mathrm{router}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{M}Q_{0}^{\top}v+\tfrac{1}{M}(\Delta_{2}Q)^{\top}v,\qquad v_{i}:=\dot{\phi}_{i,2}(h_{2}^{3,i})^{\top}(W_{2}^{4})^{\top}

\displaystyle\in\Theta\bigl(\sigma_{Q}\|v\|/M\bigr)\text{ random direction}+\Theta\bigl(\langle\partial f_{1}/\partial\phi,v\rangle/M\bigr)\,h_{1}^{1}\text{ along }h_{1}^{1}

\displaystyle\quad(Q_{0}\text{ piece via cross-layer CLT};\ \Delta_{2}Q\text{ piece coherent under MSSP})

\displaystyle=\Theta(1/\sqrt{N}\cdot\sqrt{M}\cdot 1/M)=\Theta(1/\sqrt{MN})

\displaystyle\quad(\sigma_{Q}^{2}=1/N;\ \|v\|^{2}\in\Theta(M)\text{ via }v_{i}\in\Theta(1)\text{ from (R3$s$-B3.4)};

\displaystyle\qquad\langle\partial f_{1}/\partial\phi,v\rangle\in\Theta(1)\text{ via MSSP coherence}).

(R3
s
-B3.6)

\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{1}=(\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{1})_{\mathrm{exp}}+(\partial f_{2}/\partial h_{2}^{1})_{\mathrm{router}}\in\Theta(1/N).

Appendix G
DMFT Analysis for Regime I

G.1
Setup

We use the following architecture:

\displaystyle h_{\mu}^{1}

\displaystyle=W^{1}x_{\mu}

(G.1)

\displaystyle\psi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}Q\sigma(h^{1}_{\mu})

\displaystyle\phi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\text{softmax}(\psi_{\mu})

\displaystyle h^{2}_{\mu,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{2}_{i}\sigma(h_{\mu}^{1}),

\displaystyle h^{\ell+1}_{\mu,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{\ell+1}_{i}\sigma(h_{\mu,i}^{\ell}),\quad\ell\in\{2,3,...,L-1\}

\displaystyle h^{L}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{M}\phi_{\mu}^{i}\cdot h_{\mu,i}^{L}

\displaystyle h^{L+1}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}w^{L+1\mathsf{T}}h^{L}_{\mu}

\displaystyle f_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma}h_{\mu}^{L+1}

\displaystyle w_{\alpha}^{L+1}(0)

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)

\displaystyle W_{\alpha\beta,i}^{\ell}(0)

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)\text{ for }\ell\in\{2,3,...,L\},i\in\{1,2,...,M\}

\displaystyle W_{\alpha\beta}^{1}(0)

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta}(0)

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle=\eta_{0}\gamma^{2}

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\gamma_{0}\sqrt{N}

\displaystyle\eta_{0},\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle\sim O(1)

x_{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
is the embedding vector of input
\mu\in\{1,2,...,P\}
,
\psi_{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
gives the router’s preferences for each of the
M
experts,
\phi_{\mu}^{i}
denotes the
i^{th}
component of
\phi_{\mu}
, for
i\in\{1,2,...,M\}
,
\sigma
is an element-wise nonlinearity,
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}
,
w^{4}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
,
W^{2}_{i},W^{3}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
,
W^{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times D}
, the preactivations
h
are in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
, except for
h_{\mu}^{4}
, which is a scalar. We define
\text{softmax}:\mathbb{R}^{p}\to(0,1)^{p},\,p>1
as the function which takes a tuple
\bm{z}=(z_{1},z_{2},...,z_{p})\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and computes each component of the vector
\text{softmax}(\bm{z})\in(0,1)^{p}
with
[\text{softmax}(\bm{z})]_{i}=\frac{e^{\beta z_{i}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{p}e^{\beta z_{j}}}
, where
T=\frac{1}{\beta}
is the temperature of the softmax. We will be interested in the low temperature limit
\beta\to\infty
, where softmax implements Top-1.

We denote for brevity
\bm{\theta}=\text{Vec}\{W^{1},W^{\ell}_{i},w^{L+1},Q\}_{i,\ell}
, for
i\in\{1,2,...,M\}
and
\ell\in\{2,3,...,L\}
, and define the gradients

\displaystyle g_{\mu}^{l}=\sqrt{N}\frac{\partial h_{\mu}^{L+1}}{\partial h_{\mu}^{l}},\text{ }l\in\text{Vec}\{1,(2,i),(3,i),...,(L,i),L,L+1\}_{i}

(G.2)

\displaystyle g_{\mu}^{\phi}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{\partial h_{\mu}^{L+1}}{\partial\phi_{\mu}}

g^{1}
has two terms, as we must track the gradient flowing both through the router, and through the experts. Using
G.4
, the components of
g^{\phi}_{\mu}
are

(g^{\phi}_{\mu})_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{\partial h_{\mu}^{L+1}}{\partial\phi_{\mu}^{i}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{\partial h_{\mu}^{L+1}}{\partial\bm{h}^{L}_{\mu}}\cdot\frac{\partial\bm{h}_{\mu}^{L}}{\partial\phi_{\mu}^{i}}=\frac{1}{N}\bm{g}^{L}\cdot\bm{h}_{\mu,i}^{L}=O(1).

(G.3)

In addition, we define the following
pre-gradient fields
which are defined as the gradients with respect to the activations instead of the pre-activations. For linear layers, they clearly reduce to the gradient fields.

\displaystyle g^{L}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=z^{L}_{\mu}=w^{L+1}

(G.4)

\displaystyle g^{L,i}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=z_{\mu,i}^{L}=g^{L}_{\mu}\phi^{i}_{\mu}

\displaystyle g_{\mu,i}^{\ell}

\displaystyle=\dot{\sigma}(h_{\mu,i}^{\ell})\odot z_{\mu,i}^{\ell},\quad z_{\mu,i}^{\ell}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W_{i}^{\ell+1\mathsf{T}}g_{\mu,i}^{\ell+1},\quad\ell\in\{2,3,4,...,L-1\},i\in\{1,2,...,M\}

\displaystyle(g_{\mu}^{\phi})_{i}

\displaystyle=(z_{\mu}^{\phi})_{i}=\frac{1}{N}g^{L}_{\mu}\cdot h_{\mu,i}^{L},\quad i\in\{1,2,...,M\}

\displaystyle g_{\mu}^{1}

\displaystyle=\dot{\sigma}(h^{1}_{\mu})\odot z_{\mu}^{1},\quad z_{\mu}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{M}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{2\mathsf{T}}_{i}g_{\mu,i}^{2}+Q^{\top}g^{\phi}_{\mu}

For convenience, we define
\tilde{z}_{\mu}^{\phi}:=Q^{\top}g^{\phi}_{\mu}
and
\tilde{z}_{\mu,i}^{1}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{2\mathsf{T}}_{i}g_{\mu,i}^{2}
. Then we have
z_{\mu}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{M}\tilde{z}_{\mu,i}^{1}
and
z_{\mu}^{\phi}=\tilde{z}_{\mu}^{\phi}
.

We define also the following feature and gradient kernels (The gradient kernels may be considered to be defined in terms of the z-objects via
G.4
) :

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{0}

\displaystyle:=x_{\mu}\cdot x_{\nu},

(G.5)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{1}(s,t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}\,\sigma(h_{\mu}^{1}(s))\cdot\sigma(h_{\nu}^{1}(t)),

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(s,t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}\,\sigma\!\big(h_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(s)\big)\cdot\sigma\!\big(h_{\nu,i}^{\ell}(t)\big)\qquad\ell\in\{2,3,...,L-1\},

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{L}(s,t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}\,h_{\mu}^{L}(s)\cdot h_{\nu}^{L}(t)

\displaystyle G_{\mu\nu}^{1}(s,t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}g^{1}_{\mu}(s)\cdot g^{1}_{\nu}(t),

\displaystyle G_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(s,t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}g_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(s)\cdot g_{\nu,i}^{\ell}(t)\qquad\ell\in\{2,3,...,L\}

\displaystyle G_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(s,t)

\displaystyle:=g^{\phi}_{\mu}(s)g^{\phi}_{\nu}(t)

All of these kernels are normalised to be order unity in
N
.

G.2
Dynamics

We train using gradient flow with learning rate
\eta
on the loss function

\mathcal{L}=\frac{1}{P}\sum_{\mu=1}^{P}\ell(f_{\mu},y_{\mu})

(G.6)

This induces the following dynamics:

\displaystyle\frac{d\theta}{dt}=\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma}{P}\sum_{\mu}\Delta_{\mu}\frac{\partial h_{\mu}^{L+1}}{\partial\theta}

(G.7)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mu}=-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial f_{\mu}}

In particular, for a MSE loss
\mathcal{L}=\frac{1}{P}\sum_{\nu}(y_{\nu}-f_{\nu})^{2}
, we have
\Delta_{\nu}=2(y_{\nu}-f_{\nu})
.

The logits update as

\frac{df_{\mu}(t)}{dt}=\frac{\partial f_{\mu}(t)}{\partial\theta}\cdot\frac{d\theta}{dt}=\frac{\eta}{P}\sum_{\alpha}\Delta_{\alpha}\,K^{\mathrm{NTK}}_{\mu\alpha}(t,t)

(G.8)

\text{where}\quad K^{\mathrm{NTK}}_{\mu\alpha}(t,s)\equiv\frac{\partial f_{\mu}(t)}{\partial\theta}\cdot\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}(s)}{\partial\theta}\quad\text{is the neural tangent kernel.}

(G.9)

We can derive from this definition and the repeated use of chain rule that

\gamma^{2}K^{NTK}_{\mu\nu}(t,t)=G_{\mu\nu}^{1}\Phi^{0}_{\mu\nu}+\sum_{i=1}^{M}[G_{\mu\nu,i}^{2}\Phi^{1}_{\mu\nu}+\sum_{\ell=3}^{L}G_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}\Phi^{\ell-1}_{\mu\nu,i}]+\Phi^{L}_{\mu\nu}+G^{\phi}_{\mu\nu}\Phi^{1}_{\mu\nu}

(G.10)

Hence

\displaystyle\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}f_{\mu}(t)}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{0}}{P}\sum_{\nu=1}^{P}\Big[G_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,t)\Phi^{0}_{\mu\nu}+\sum_{i=1}^{M}[G_{\mu\nu,i}^{2}(t,t)\Phi^{1}_{\mu\nu}(t,t)+\sum_{\ell=3}^{L}G_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,t)\Phi^{\ell-1}_{\mu\nu,i}(t,t)]+\Phi^{L}_{\mu\nu}(t,t)

(G.11)

\displaystyle+G^{\phi}_{\mu\nu}(t,t)\Phi^{1}_{\mu\nu}(t,t)\Big]\Delta_{\nu}(t)

Using the learning dynamics
G.7
, we find for the weight matrices

\frac{dW^{1}(t)}{dt}=\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\sum_{\mu}\Delta_{\mu}\,g_{\mu}^{1}(t)x_{\mu}^{\top}

(G.12)

\frac{d\,W^{2}_{i}(t)}{dt}=\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{\sqrt{N}\,P}\sum_{\mu}\Delta_{\mu}\,g_{\mu,i}^{2}\sigma\!\big(h_{\mu}^{1}\big)^{\top}

(G.13)

\frac{d\,W^{\ell}_{i}(t)}{dt}=\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{\sqrt{N}\,P}\sum_{\mu}\Delta_{\mu}\,g_{\mu,i}^{\ell}\sigma\big(h_{\mu,i}^{\ell-1}\big)^{\top},\quad\ell\in\{3,4,...,L\}

(G.14)

\frac{d\,w^{L+1}(t)}{dt}=\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}\sqrt{N}}{P}\sum_{\mu}\Delta_{\mu}\frac{\partial h_{\mu}^{L+1}}{\partial w^{L+1}}=\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\sqrt{N}\sum_{\mu}\Delta_{\mu}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}h_{\mu}^{L}=\frac{\gamma_{0}\eta_{0}}{P}\sum_{\mu}\Delta_{\mu}h_{\mu}^{L}

(G.15)

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{dQ(t)}{dt}}_{\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\sum_{\mu=1}^{P}\Delta_{\mu}(t)\underbrace{g^{\phi}_{\mu}(t)}_{\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times 1}}\underbrace{[\sigma(h^{1}_{\mu}(t))]^{\top}}_{\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times N}}

(G.16)

We can now integrate these expressions and use the definitions of the pre-activations from
G.1
to obtain:

\displaystyle W^{1}(t)

\displaystyle=W^{1}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\!ds\,\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\sum_{\mu}\Delta_{\mu}\,g_{\mu}^{1}(s)\cdot x_{\mu}

(G.17)

\displaystyle\implies h_{\mu}^{1}(t)

\displaystyle=W^{1}(0)x_{\mu}+\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}\!ds\sum_{\nu}\Delta_{\nu}\,g_{\nu}^{1}(s)\,\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{0}

\displaystyle W^{2}_{i}(t)

\displaystyle=W^{2}_{i}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\!ds\,\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{\sqrt{N}P}\sum_{\mu}\Delta_{\mu}\,g_{\mu,i}^{2}(s)\sigma(h_{\mu}^{1}(s))^{\top}

\displaystyle\implies{h^{2}_{\mu,i}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{2}_{i}(0)\sigma(h_{\mu}^{1}(t))+\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}\!ds\sum_{\nu}\Delta_{\nu}\,g_{\nu,i}^{2}(s)\,\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{1}(s,t)

\displaystyle W^{\ell}_{i}(t)

\displaystyle=W^{\ell}_{i}(0)+\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{\sqrt{N}P}\int_{0}^{t}\!ds\sum_{\mu}\Delta_{\mu}\,g_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(s)\,\sigma\!\big(h_{\mu,i}^{\ell-1}\big)^{\!T}

\displaystyle\implies h_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{\ell}_{i}(0)\,\sigma\!\big(h_{\mu,i}^{\ell-1}(t)\big)+\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}\!ds\sum_{\nu}\Delta_{\nu}\,g_{\nu,i}^{\ell}(s)\,\Phi_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell-1}(s,t),\quad\ell\in\{3,4,...,L\}

\displaystyle Q(t)

\displaystyle=Q(0)+\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}\!ds\,\sum_{\mu=1}^{P}\Delta_{\mu}(s)\,g^{\phi}_{\mu}(s)\,\bigl(h_{\mu}^{1}(s)\bigr)^{\top}

\displaystyle\implies\psi_{\mu}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\,Q(0)\,\sigma(h_{\mu}^{1}(t))+\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}\!ds\,\sum_{\nu=1}^{P}\Delta_{\nu}(s)\,g^{\phi}_{\nu}(s)\,\Phi^{1}_{\mu\nu}(s,t)

This is useful, because we hope to average over the weights at initialization to obtain expressions in terms of only features.
These expressions
G.17
motivate the definition of the following fields:

\displaystyle\chi_{\mu}^{1}=W^{1}(0)x_{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}

(G.18)

\displaystyle\chi_{\mu,i}^{2}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{2}_{i}(0)\sigma(h_{\mu}^{1}(t))\in\mathbb{R}^{N}

\displaystyle\chi_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{\ell}_{i}(0)\sigma(h_{\mu,i}^{\ell-1}(t))\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\quad\ell\in\{3,4,...,L\}

\displaystyle\chi^{\phi}_{\mu}(t)=\frac{1}{N}Q(0)\sigma(h_{\mu}^{1}(t))\in\mathbb{R}^{M}

We repeat the analysis of
G.17
for the gradient fields which are defined as
z_{\mu}^{\ell}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\,W^{\ell+1}(t)^{\top}\,g_{\mu}^{\ell+1}(t)
, where
\ell
indexes the layer, and may involve an expert index too, and
z^{\phi}_{\mu}(t)=Q(t)^{\top}g^{\phi}_{\mu}(t)
: In particular, we have
\forall i\in\{1,2,...,M\}

\displaystyle z_{\mu}^{\phi}(t)

\displaystyle=\xi^{\phi}_{\mu}(t)+\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}ds\sum_{\nu=1}^{P}\Delta_{\nu}(s)\sigma(h^{1}_{\nu}(s))G_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)

(G.19)

\displaystyle z_{\mu,i}^{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\xi_{\mu}^{1,i}(t)+\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}\!ds\sum_{\nu}\Delta_{\nu}\,\sigma(h^{1}_{\nu}(s))\,G_{\mu\nu,i}^{2}(s,t)

\displaystyle z_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)

\displaystyle=\xi_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)+\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}\!ds\sum_{\nu}\Delta_{\nu}\,\sigma(h^{\ell}_{\nu,i}(s))\,G_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell+1}(s,t)\quad\ell\in\{2,3,...,L-1\}

\displaystyle z_{\mu,i}^{L}

\displaystyle=\phi_{\mu}^{i}z^{L}_{\mu}

\displaystyle z_{\mu}^{L}(t)

\displaystyle=w_{\mu}^{L+1}(t)=\xi_{\mu}^{L}(t)+\frac{\gamma_{0}\eta_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}ds\sum_{\nu}\Delta_{\nu}h_{\nu}^{L},

where we define the following fields:

\displaystyle\xi_{\mu}^{\phi}(t)=Q(0)^{\top}g_{\mu}^{\phi}(t)

(G.20)

\displaystyle\xi_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{\ell+1}_{i}(0)^{\mathsf{T}}g_{\mu,i}^{\ell+1}(t)\quad\ell\in\{1,2,...,L-1\}

\displaystyle\xi_{\mu}^{L}=w^{L+1}(0)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)

Note that
\xi^{L}_{\mu}
is in fact time-independent, and equal to the initialization of
w^{L+1}
. We know that this object has entries distributed as
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, and we will also recover this from the DMFT for completeness.

Note also that the final gradient is
g^{4}_{\mu}=\sqrt{N}
, so we can think of the final equation in
G.19
as involving the identity kernel.

Finally, we write

\displaystyle\big[g^{\phi}\big]_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}g_{n}^{L}\,\big[h_{i}^{L}\big]_{n}=\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{n,m=1}^{N}g_{n}^{L}\big[W_{i}^{L}\big]_{nm}\,\sigma\!\big(\big[h_{i}^{L-1}\big]_{m}\big)

(G.21)

\displaystyle\big[g^{\phi}_{\mu}(t)\big]_{i}

\displaystyle=\xi^{g^{\phi}}_{\mu i}(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}ds\sum_{\nu}\Delta_{\nu}(s)\sum_{n,m=1}^{N}g_{\mu n}^{L}(t)g_{\nu n}^{L}(s)\phi_{\nu}^{i}(s)\sigma\!\big(\big[h_{\mu i}^{L-1}(t)\big]_{m}\big)\,\sigma\!\big(\big[h_{\nu i}^{L-1}(s)\big]_{m}\big)

\displaystyle=\xi^{g^{\phi}}_{\mu i}(t)+\frac{\eta_{0}\gamma_{0}}{P}\int_{0}^{t}ds\sum_{\nu}\Delta_{\nu}(s)\,G_{\mu\nu}^{L}(t,s)\,{\Phi}_{\mu\nu}^{L-1}(t,s)\,\phi^{i}_{\nu}(s)

Where we define the field

\displaystyle\xi^{g^{\phi}}_{\mu i}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\,g^{L\,\top}_{\mu}(t)\,W_{i}^{L}(0)\,\sigma\!\big(h_{\mu i}^{L-1}(t)\big).

(G.22)

G.3
Mean field theory

If we can show that the kernels defined above concentrate, then our DMFT will be determined by the stochastic fields
\mathcal{F}=\{\chi_{\mu}^{1},\chi_{\mu,i}^{2},\chi_{\mu,i}^{3},...,\chi_{\mu,i}^{L}\chi^{\phi}_{\mu},\xi_{\mu,i}^{1},\xi_{\mu,i}^{2},...,\xi_{\mu,i}^{L-1}\xi_{\mu}^{L},\xi^{\phi}_{\mu}\}_{i\in\{1,2,...,M\}}
. Therefore to construct the DMFT, we must compute the moment generating functional of these stochastic processes.

For brevity, we define, alongside the set
\mathcal{F}
of fields, the set
\mathcal{S}
of sources, which contains a corresponding source
j
(or
v
) for every
\chi
(or
\xi
) in
\mathcal{F}
. So
\mathcal{S}=\{j_{\mu}^{1},j_{\mu,i}^{2},j_{\mu,i}^{3},...,j_{\mu,i}^{L}j^{\phi}_{\mu},v_{\mu,i}^{1},v_{\mu,i}^{2},...,v_{\mu,i}^{L-1}v_{\mu}^{L},v^{\phi}_{\mu}\}_{i\in\{1,2,...,M\}}
. This notation is constructed so that we can write
\mathcal{F}\cdot\mathcal{S}
to denote
j_{\mu}^{1}(t)\cdot\chi_{\mu}^{1}(t)+j_{\mu,i}^{2}(t)\cdot\chi_{\mu,i}^{2}(t)+...+v_{\mu}^{\phi}(t)\cdot\xi_{\mu}^{\phi}(t)
. Note that
v^{\phi}_{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
, but all the other dummy variables are in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
. We also define
\theta_{0}=\mathrm{Vec}\!\left\{W^{1}(0),\,W^{2}_{i}(0),...,W^{L}_{i}(0)\,,w^{L+1}(0),\,Q(0)\right\}_{i\in\{1,...,M\}}.

The MGF with which we are concerned is therefore

\displaystyle Z\!\left[\{j,v\}\right]\Bigg\langle\exp\!\Bigg(\sum_{\mu}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!dt\,\mathcal{F}\cdot\mathcal{S}\Bigg)\Bigg\rangle_{\theta_{0}}

(G.23)

v^{\phi}_{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
, but all the other dummy variables are in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
.

In order to integrate, we enforce definitions of our random fields using resolutions of the identity to obtain:

\displaystyle Z=

\displaystyle\Bigg\langle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{8N}}\prod_{\mu=1}^{P}\prod_{i=1}^{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\;\Bigg\{\exp\!\Bigg(\sum_{\mu}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!dt\,\mathcal{F}\cdot\mathcal{S}\Bigg)+

(G.24)

\displaystyle\int d\mathcal{F}\exp[i\Bigg(\hat{\chi}_{\mu}^{1}\!\cdot\!\big(\chi_{\mu}^{1}-W^{1}(0)x_{\mu}\big)+\hat{\chi}_{\mu,i}^{2}\!\cdot\!\Big(\chi_{\mu,i}^{2}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{2}_{i}(0)\sigma(h_{\mu}^{1}(t))\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell=3}^{L}\hat{\chi}_{\mu,i}^{\ell}\!\cdot\!\Big(\chi_{\mu,i}^{\ell}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{\ell}_{i}(0)\sigma(h_{\mu,i}^{\ell-1}(t))\Big)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\hat{\xi}_{\mu,i}^{\ell}\!\cdot\!\Big(\xi_{\mu,i}^{\ell}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{\ell+1}_{i}(0)^{\!\top}g_{\mu,i}^{\ell+1}(t)\Big)

\displaystyle+\hat{\xi}_{\mu}^{L}\!\cdot\!\Big(\xi_{\mu}^{L}-w^{L+1}(0)^{\!\top})+\hat{\chi}^{\phi}_{\mu}\cdot\bigg(\chi^{\phi}_{\mu}-\frac{1}{N}Q(0)\sigma(h^{1}_{\mu}(t))\bigg)+\hat{\xi}^{\phi}_{\mu}\cdot\bigg(\xi^{\phi}_{\mu}-Q(0)^{\top}g^{\phi}_{\mu}(t)\bigg)

\displaystyle+\hat{\xi}^{g^{\phi}}\,\cdot\,\left(\xi^{g^{\phi}}_{\mu}-\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}g^{L\top}_{\mu}W^{L}_{i}(0)\sigma(h_{\mu,i}^{L-1})\right)\Bigg)\Bigg]\Bigg\}\Bigg\rangle_{\theta_{0}}

We then integrate using the fact that for a Gaussian variable
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
,
\langle e^{-ia\cdot z}\rangle_{z}\propto e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}|a|^{2}}
to obtain

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle\propto\prod_{\mu=1}^{P}\prod_{\nu=1}^{P}\prod_{i=1}^{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\int dt\int d\mathcal{F}

(G.25)

\displaystyle\times\exp\{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mu,\nu=1}^{P}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\bigg[\hat{\chi}_{\mu}^{1}(t)\!\cdot\!\hat{\chi}_{\nu}^{1}(s)\,\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{0}(t,s)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\chi}_{\mu,i}^{2}(t)\!\cdot\!\hat{\chi}_{\nu,i}^{2}(s)+\frac{1}{N}\hat{\chi}^{\phi}_{\mu}(t)\cdot\hat{\chi}^{\phi}_{\nu}(s))\,\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)+\sum_{\ell=3}^{L}\hat{\chi}_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(s)\!\cdot\!\hat{\chi}_{\nu,i}^{\ell}(t)\,\Phi_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell-1}(s,t)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L}\hat{\xi}_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(s)\!\cdot\!\hat{\xi}_{\nu,i}^{\ell}(t)\,G_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(s,t)+\hat{\xi}_{\mu}^{L+1}(s)\!\cdot\!\hat{\xi}_{\nu}^{L+1}(t)+\hat{\xi}_{\mu}^{\phi}(s)\!\cdot\!\hat{\xi}_{\nu}^{\phi}(t)G^{\phi}_{\mu\nu}(t,s)\bigg]

\displaystyle-i\sum_{\mu,\nu=1}^{P}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\,\left[\sum_{\ell=2}^{L}\hat{\chi}_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)\cdot g_{\nu,i}^{\ell}(t)A_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,t)+\hat{\chi}_{\mu}^{\phi}(t)\cdot g_{\nu}^{\phi}(t)A_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,t)\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mu=1}^{P}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\Bigg[\sum_{\ell=2}^{L-1}\Big((j_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)+i\hat{\chi}_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t))\cdot\chi_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)+(v_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)+i\hat{\xi}_{\mu,i}^{\ell})\cdot\xi_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)\Big)

\displaystyle+(j_{\mu}^{1}(t)+i\hat{\chi}_{\mu}^{1}(t))\cdot\chi_{\mu}^{1}(t)\quad+(v_{\mu}^{L}+i\hat{\xi}^{L}_{\mu}(t))\cdot\xi^{L}_{\mu}(t)

\displaystyle+(j_{\mu,i}^{L}(t)+i\hat{\chi}_{\mu,i}^{L}(t))\cdot\chi_{\mu,i}^{L}(t)+(v_{\mu,i}^{1}(t)+i\hat{\xi}_{\mu,i}^{1}(t))\cdot\xi_{\mu,i}^{1}(t)+(v_{\mu}^{\phi}+i\hat{\xi}^{\phi}_{\mu}(t))\cdot\xi^{\phi}_{\mu}(t)

\displaystyle+(j_{\mu}^{\phi}+i\hat{\chi}^{\phi}_{\mu}(t))\cdot\chi^{\phi}_{\mu}(t)+(v_{\mu}^{g^{\phi}}+i\hat{\xi}^{g^{\phi}}_{\mu}(t))\cdot\xi^{g^{\phi}}_{\mu}(t)\Bigg]\Bigg\}.

Where we have defined the kernels

A_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(s,t)=\begin{cases}-\frac{i}{N}\hat{\xi}_{\mu,i}^{1}(s)\cdot\sigma(h^{1}_{\nu}(t))\quad\ell=2\\
-\frac{i}{N}\hat{\xi}_{\mu,i}^{\ell-1}(s)\cdot\sigma(h^{\ell-1}_{\nu,i}(t))\quad\ell\in\{3,4,...,L\}\end{cases}

(G.26)

A_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(s,t)=-\frac{i}{N}\hat{\xi}_{\mu}^{\phi}(s)\cdot\sigma(h^{1}_{\nu}(t))

(G.27)

There are also three terms arising from the coupling of
\xi^{g^{\phi}}
to itself and to the forwards and backwards fields at layer L. These vanish as
\frac{1}{N}
, so have been excluded from
G.25
for brevity.

The
\Phi
and
G
kernels are required for computing the evolution of the function output, via the NTK. The kernels
A
are not involved in the NTK, but rather arise from the coupling of the fields across a single layer’s initial weight matrix. We now enforce the definitions of all three types of kernels using integral representations of Dirac delta-functions. In particular, for each pair
\mu,\nu
of samples, each expert
i
and each pair
t,s
of times, we multiply by

1=\int\frac{d\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{0}(t,s)\,d\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu}^{0}(t,s)}{2\pi i}\exp\!\left[\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu}^{0}(t,s)\left(\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{0}(t,s)-x_{\mu}\cdot x_{\nu}\right)\right]

(G.28)

1=\int\frac{d\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)\,d\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)}{2\pi i\,N^{-1}}\exp\!\left[\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)\left(N\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)-\sigma(h_{\mu}^{1}(t))\cdot\sigma\!\big(h_{\nu}^{1}(s)\big)\right)\right]

(G.29)

\displaystyle 1=\int\frac{d\Phi_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)\,d\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)}{2\pi i\,N^{-1}}\exp\!\Big[\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)\big(N\Phi_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)-

\displaystyle\sigma\!\big(h_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)\big)\cdot\sigma\!\big(h_{\nu,i}^{\ell}(s)\big)\big)\Big]

(G.30)

\displaystyle\ell\in\{2,3,...,L-1\}

1=\int\frac{d\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{L}(t,s)\,d\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu}^{L}(t,s)}{2\pi i\,N^{-1}}\exp\!\left[\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu}^{L}(t,s)\left(N\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{L}(t,s)-h_{\mu}^{L}(t)\cdot h_{\nu}^{L}(s)\right)\right]

(G.31)

1=\int\frac{dG_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)\,d\hat{G}_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)}{2\pi i\,N^{-1}}\exp\!\left[\hat{G}_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)\left(NG_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)-g_{\mu}^{1}(t)\cdot g_{\nu}^{1}(s)\right)\right]

(G.32)

\displaystyle 1=\int\frac{dG_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)\,d\hat{G}_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)}{2\pi i\,N^{-1}}\exp\!\Big[\hat{G}_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)\big(NG_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)-

\displaystyle g_{\mu,i}^{\ell}(t)\cdot g_{\nu,i}^{\ell}(s)\big)\Big]

(G.33)

\displaystyle\ell\in\{2,3,...,L\}

1=\int\frac{dA_{\mu\nu,i}^{2}(t,s)\,dB_{\mu\nu,i}^{2}(t,s)}{2\pi i\,N^{-1}}\exp\!\left[-B_{\mu\nu,i}^{2}(t,s)\left(NA_{\mu\nu,i}^{2}(t,s)+i\hat{\xi}_{\,i}^{1}(t)\cdot\sigma(h_{\nu}^{1}(s))\right)\right]

(G.34)

1=\int\frac{dA_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)\,dB_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)}{2\pi i\,N^{-1}}\exp\!\left[-B_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)\left(NA_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)+i\hat{\xi}_{\mu}^{\phi}(t)\cdot\sigma(h_{\nu}^{1}(s))\right)\right]

(G.35)

\displaystyle 1=\int\frac{dA_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)\,dB_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)}{2\pi i\,N^{-1}}\exp\!\Big[-B_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)\big(NA_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)+i\hat{\xi}_{\mu,i}^{\ell-1}(t)

\displaystyle\cdot\sigma(h_{\nu,i}^{\ell-1}(s))\big)\Big]

(G.36)

\displaystyle\ell\in\{3,4,...,L\}

1=\int\frac{dG_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)\,d\hat{G}_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)}{2\pi i}\exp\!\left[\hat{G}_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)\left(G_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)+g_{\mu}^{\phi}(t)\cdot g_{\nu}^{\phi}(t)\right)\right]

(G.37)

We call the conjugate kernel of
A

B
, rather than
\hat{A}
. This is because it will turn out to be equal to
\hat{\chi}\cdot g
, which appears in the gradient stream update equation in the final DMFT.

Multiplying in all these factors of unity, we arrive at a partition function which factorises over each of the N sites in each hidden layer. There are two things to note here:

1.

We want to treat g,h as scalars, so each of the resolutions of the identity
G.28
to
G.37
above will be duplicated N times leading to an extensive factor N, which pulls through to the front of the exponential, since all the neurons in a given layer are indistinguishable in the limit, so have the same
\Phi
or
G
. That is, we can write for instance

1=\int\frac{dG_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)\,d\hat{G}_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)}{2\pi i\,N^{-1}}\exp\!\left[N\hat{G}_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)\left(G_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)-g_{\mu}^{1}(t)g_{\nu}^{1}(s)\right)\right]

(G.38)

Where
g^{1}_{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}
are the components of the former vector of the same name, which are considered statistically indistinguishable in the limit.
The exception to this however is
G.37
, since
g^{\phi}_{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
. M is not being taken to the infinite limit, so we cannot consider the M components of
g^{\phi}_{\mu}
to be indistinguishable. Rather we must sum over them:

1=\int\frac{dG_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)\,d\hat{G}_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)}{2\pi i\,}\exp\!\left[\sum_{\beta=1}^{M}\hat{G}_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)\left(G_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)+g_{\mu,\beta}^{\phi}(t)g_{\nu,\beta}^{\phi}(t)\right)\right].

(G.39)

2.

Each term in the argument of the exponential in
G.25
is a dot product of two vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
, so contains N terms which are identical in the limit. We can therefore bring out a factor N here too. Once again, the exception is the term
(j_{\mu}^{\phi}(t)+i\hat{\chi}^{\phi}_{\mu}(t))\chi_{\mu}^{\phi}(t)
, which is repeated M times. Therefore when we bring a factor N out front of the action, we must include a prefactor
\frac{M}{N}
with this term.

Define the set
\mathcal{K}
of all order parameters. This includes all the kernels defined above
.

\displaystyle Z\propto\int

\displaystyle\prod_{\mu,\nu,t,s,i}d\mathcal{K}\,\exp({NS[\{\hat\Phi,\Phi,\hat G,G,A,B\}]})

(G.40)

Where the DMFT action
S
is
O(1)
, and has the form

\adjustbox{maxwidth=}{\begin{aligned} S[\{\hat{\Phi},\Phi,\hat{G},G,A,B\}]&=\sum_{\mu,\nu}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!dt\int_{0}^{\infty}\!ds\Big[\hat{G}_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)G_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L}\hat{G}_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)G_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)\\
&\quad+\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{1}(t,s)+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L-1}\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu,i}^{2}(t,s)\Phi_{\mu\nu,i}^{2}(t,s)+\hat{\Phi}_{\mu\nu}^{L}(t,s)\Phi_{\mu\nu}^{L}(t,s)\\
&\quad-\sum_{L=2}^{L}B_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)A_{\mu\nu,i}^{\ell}(t,s)+\frac{1}{N}\hat{G}_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)G_{\mu\nu}^{\phi}(t,s)-B^{\phi}_{\mu\nu}(t,s)A^{\phi}_{\mu\nu}(t,s)\Big]\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\ln\mathcal{Z}_{N}[\Phi,\hat{\Phi},G,\hat{G},A,B,j_{\alpha},v_{\alpha}]+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\beta=1}^{M}\ln\mathcal{Z}_{M}[\Phi^{1},\hat{G}^{\phi},j^{\phi}_{\beta}]\end{aligned}}

(G.41)

such that it consists of inner products of the order parameters
\{\Phi,G,A\}
and their duals
\{\hat{\Phi},\hat{G},B\}
, in addition to the single-site MGFs
